[[Note: The following table of contents is not the final text because rationalisation and editorial corrections are pending]] We are entering the agenda of the LEGISLATIVE WORK Continue the discussion of the draft law of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs: "For the Protection of Labour - Establishment of an Independent Labour Inspection Authority" - Settlement of the Convention 190 of the International Labour Organisation for the Elimination of Violence and Harassment in the World of Work - Clearing of the Convention 187 of the International Labour Organisation on the Framework for the Promotion of Safety and Health at Work - Incorporation of Directive (EU) 2019/1158 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 June 2019 on the balance between professional and private life". Mr. George Logiadis has the floor for seven minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u2	Thank you, Mr President. I remember my father saying to me: 'A society is like a net, like a chain, with its weakest link'. This weakest link must be strengthened, supported and developed. By definition, Labour Law protects the weakest, i.e. the worker and I speak as an employer. A major mistake in today's draft law is that it treats both parties as equals the worker and the employer. Another problem is that, up to now, anyone who worked under a semi-employment scheme of less than eight hours a day should have continuous working hours. Now he'll be able to break six or four hours while he was working, in the day. The Labour Inspectorate has been devalued, there is not even to check that today's eighth day is in force. Not much more for what is set out in your current draft law. The Labour Inspectorate must be strengthened and not become an independent authority. Collective agreements must, of course, apply and apply to everyone so that there is no black and undeclared work, which creates unfair competition, destroys businesses and workers. If the worker is not happy he will not yield and in the end society is falling apart and degraded. Both the Association of Judges and Prosecutors, which stands in particular at the abolition of the eighth and Sunday holidays and the Scientific Committee of the Parliament, are contrary to this draft law. In the context of the memoranda you have signed because of overtaxation in order to achieve primary surpluses, any revenues are continuously reduced. When super-private debts, private and public debts, are cumulated, high taxes are choking productive investment and poverty is alternatively removed by many wage earners, it is impossible to protect themselves. In such a environment, deregulation of the labour market, for example, with individual contracts attempted by the government, further reduces the share of wages in national income. The reduction in the wage share also reduces overall demand. And the decline in total demand stimulates underdevelopment. We will have shallow entrepreneurship and low added value. Merra25 points out that no labour law will protect the job unless it is accompanied by debt haircuts. With the further reduction of labour costs aimed at the draft law, the fifth memorandum is already here. It had to be done exactly the opposite. Education and support for workers, stable working framework, decent final income available. The state with its policies must grow the pie and not shrink it and create social tensions. In conclusion, I would also like to refer to tourism as a Member of the House in our heavy industry. The Hotel Officers' Association of Heraklion Prefecture on 13-21 this year signed with the Heraklion Hotel Association the new local collective labour contract to ensure the conditions for the remuneration and work of the hotel staff. With a joint request from workers and employers to the Ministry of Labour and Social Security, they asked for the declaration of the new local collective labour agreement as generally compulsory, thus avoiding negative impacts on the competitiveness of the businesses that have to do with labour costs, on the one hand, on the one hand, to achieve fair support for the standard of living of the hotel workers whose vast majority work in the seasonal operating units of the prefecture and the whole of Greece. In repeated letters sent to the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs and the Ministry's General Secretariat, as well as to the relevant directorates, there is so far no information about what will happen. They therefore request that the new local collective labour agreement be declared generally acceptable. Thank you very much.

The fact is that the General Hospital of Xanthi, although standing up to the difficult moments of the pandemic, is now being driven to the forefront of the collapse. The hospital's intensified treatment facility from the beginning of the year is not working. The entire hospital has a available anesthesiologist and the shortages in medical staff overall reach 60%. Unfortunately, it is now certain that our hospital will not withstand a possible fourth wave of pandemic. So does the government show its gratitude to Xanthi Hospital, to his medical and nursing staff? In this difficult treaty, the state had to shield and defend the health system, support doctors and through them and citizens. Instead, all he knows to do is weaken the National Health System and promote the General Hospital of Xanthi to the collapse. However, the Government of New Democracy has not even done anything in the other areas. On the occasion of the reduction of parliamentary procedures, there has been a large number of unpopular legislation in the economy, education, rural development and today at work. Another characteristic example is the zero support for farmers in the face of pandemic. Especially for tobacco growing, the indifference shown by the Government to strengthen our local variety "Basmas", unlike the "Virginia" variety, has very negative consequences for the continued cultivation and jobs in the region of Thrace. With regard to the economy that has been hit hugely with many padlocks in small and medium-sized enterprises, with many other companies still closed and without a clear picture of the course of tourism, the government is today going to shift the burden on the workers. Instead of supporting businesses, it claims supposedly anachronistic diseases in the field of labour, to pay workers once again. In the meantime, we have been watching a desperate attempt to become white - black and to manipulate public opinion that supposedly the bill is the charitable one and protects workers' rights. Article 22 of the Constitution for the Protection of Work is directly destroyed at a time when work should be protected as ever and in practice rather than with special purpose allowances. In addition, no government dared to harm the eighth-hour paid workers. But you, in a demonstration of insurmountable neoliberalism, do not hesitate to trespass on it. Today's bill is the final blow to the world of work, following the anti-social and anti-social policies implemented by the government on the day following the elections. At a time of deep economic and social crisis, the reduction of incomes, precariousness and unemployment began with the deterioration of the Labour Inspectorate and provided for the reduction of fines for breaches of labour law. It abolished the right to dismiss workers and dismantled the institution of collective bargaining, as well as the right to appeal for workers to arbitration. While confronted with pandemic as an opportunity to restructure the economy and deregulation of the labour market, it introduced rotating work, a reduction in wages with the 'REGION' programme and the suspension of the employment contract with an allowance of EUR 534. Furthermore, it brings its final blow by abolishing the eighth and five-day work by establishing the individual contract. Ten-hour work is now required with a reduction in wages, as the employee is paid for eight hours and there will be a set-off and leave. The adjustment of working time by an individual contract provides for a 10-hour work without additional remuneration for a 12-month reference period of increased employment with a netting capacity within six months at another period of reduced employment or a combination of reduced working and off-take times. It is obvious that the reorganization of working time by an individual contract leads to the complete dismantling of collective agreements, while the implementation of the arrangement system will be substantially the responsibility of the employer. At the same time, the number of legal overtime is increasing to fifty hours from the ninety - six hours currently in force in industry and twenty - twenty in service business. The "compulsory leave" is regulated on the initiative of the employer and the work is extended on Sundays. With all the above, the distance between the neoliberal right-wing government and SYRIZA clearly appears. Let us remember that the SYRIZA government supported workers and their income in a period of budgetary constraints. It strengthened the controls at workplaces and reversed the trend towards deregulation of the industrial relations that had been imposed during the memorandum period. This policy has contributed to a sharp decline in unemployment of 10% and at the same time, after ten years, there has been an increase in average wages in 2019. On the contrary, you come today with a bill of degradation of work and the most dramatic one, the most problematic is that you are not thinking, not only of the conditions of pandemic, but also of weakening the country from the workforce. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I shall conclude, Mr President. I come from a county that falls within the limits of the poorest - perhaps - region of the European Union. Thousands of my fellow citizens each year migrate abroad in the hope of finding jobs in other countries. The sure thing is that these deregulation initiatives will not lead to new jobs, nor will they prevent the labour force from leaking abroad. On the other hand, they are reminiscent of working-age ages and are an incentive to labour migration. SYRIZA's proposal is to build a stable labour relations framework, strengthen the negotiating power and the availability of workers' income. Everyone must be able to live from their work and plan a better future. Everyone has a right to work on decent terms. The world of work and the entire Greek people will be at odds with your destructive policy. Thank you very much.

Minister, ladies and gentlemen, the bill we are discussing today represents an important reform in the area of work and in the business sector, and it is an opportunity to discuss our ideological approaches to these issues and to see where we disagree or even agree. A prerequisite, however, for such a debate in a serious reform such as this, is the understanding and assumption of reality. Firstly, we have to admit that conditions in the world of work and business have changed. The new digital age has developed new data. The labour market and the needs of enterprises have changed. The workplace is not like it was forty years ago. And thirdly, we need to have a debate on the real arrangements in the bill, to talk about what really anticipates and about them, and to see our ideological differences. I say this because, once again, I find that SYRIZA is forming its own false reality and is opposed to the reality that it has already created. This, however, creates a dimension with the very reality and is an explanation for SYRIZA's inability to convince citizens. Of course this is reflected in all polls. I will start from this very distance from reality, ladies and gentlemen, because it makes no sense for SYRIZA to criticise provisions not provided for in the bill, but only in its own imagination. A worker's right, such as that of the working time arrangement that has been taking place in the country for thirty years in large companies, through sectoral agreements, and which also applies in Europe to the majority of European states, is denied this today. A right for the worker on his own initiative to ask him to adjust working time to his family conditions, you want to deprive him of that, and you are baptizing a friendly policy. In order to support this, you are lying, that it is allegedly abolished in eight hours and that the extra hours of work are supposed to be paid off. But tell me, ladies and gentlemen, what exactly a provision in the bill you have seen this and if you read the provision of Article 55 which explicitly provides for working time. But this seems to be common for SYRIZA to do the white one. Like also, it is common once more to pretend. You are now getting up for the settlement of working time, the arrangement that has been applied to the hotel sector, and you have recently asked that the sectoral convention be declared compulsory, that is to say, to apply to workers who may not want it. But you react to the provision of this bill, to asking as a worker whenever he wishes to regulate working time. I would remind you that the European Working Time Directive was signed on the SYRIZA, which is now being incorporated into this draft law. On SYRIZA the arrangement was implemented in a number of large companies. In 2010, the then Ministers of PASOK and more members of SYRIZA brought the law entitled "Work Safety Guarantees" provided for in Article 7 for the adjustment of working time. It was exactly the same arrangement as that provided for in this draft law. Enough of SYRIZA's hypocrisy! Ladies and gentlemen, this draft law contains provisions that show our clear ideological differences, but also the different concept of the reality and the modern needs of society and citizens. We can also discuss this on a real basis. We believe in the freedom to shape relations between employer and worker. We give the worker the opportunity, if he wishes, to shape working time differently and at the same time to protect him against abuse of behaviour by upgrading the AAR to an independent authority. We believe that the exercise of trade union rights must have limits. The protection of the trade union can't make it to immunity. The right to strike cannot go to the point of blackmailing society and afflicting citizens. It cannot go beyond justice and to circumvent judicial decisions. And it cannot be exercised through organisations - seals. That is why we are introducing a number of measures. We are introducing transparency measures to trade through the compulsory registration of trade unions in the electronic register. We are introducing a abolition of the possibility of financing trade unions from businesses, trade unions and parties. We introduce a minimum guaranteed service during a strike at the common interest to 33%. We are establishing a ban on restoring a strike that has already been judged illegal by justice, and we are introducing civil liability for trade unions who, during a strike, carry out illegal acts or practice violence. At the same time, we are creating a web of worker protection adapted to modern times and to real problems. That is how we establish the digital working card, a modern mechanism financed by the Recovery Fund, through which real overtime work will be monitored and paid and combated and undeclared work and evasion. We also have a special framework for teleworking, seeing the right to disconnect, that is, the right of the worker to spend real time with his family and his personal life. We are introducing specific provisions to deal with the phenomenon of violence and harassment at work, which we have all seen in recent years has received alarming proportions. We broaden the list of cases of invalid dismissal by protecting the father for six months from the birth of his or her child or worker exercising his or her rights and at the same time retaining all the cases already provided for in the law. We anticipate new permits for both parents and caregivers and we equate the compensation for the dismissal of the worker to that of the employee. And here are the following questions: do all these predictions agree with the opposition parties? Will they vote against these provisions too? Has the left government taken such measures, or has it not been able to legislate? Ladies and gentlemen, the protection of workers in whose name you stand is not done in words, but by actions. It is not by theories, nor by sticking to a reality of decades past, nor by the break-up of businesses, because there are no workers without businesses, nor do businesses without workers. The Government of New Democracy is taking a decisive step towards yet another reform of labour legislation by adapting it to modern social, economic and technological developments. Because progress means comprehending the era, meeting its needs and planning the future, not sticking to the logic of the past by building a virtual reality with fantasy enemies and scripts. Only then will we create a productive Greece that attracts investment and creates jobs. Only in this way will we lead the country on the road of propaganda and prosperity. So I call on you to take another step forward and vote for this draft law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u63	The Deputy of the New Democracy Mr Stavros Kalogannis is called in Stepma. Then Mr. Voutsis, Mr. Poulas, Mr. Lazarides, will speak and then the parliamentary representative of SYRIZA, Mr. Skourletis, will speak. Mr. Kalogannis, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u64	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, we are discussing the draft law on the protection of work in the conditions created in our digital age and after the pandemic of COVID -19. This is a wide-ranging legislative intervention, which combines regulations concerning international obligations of the country and the incorporation of a European directive, in the direction of modernising essential regulations of individual and collective labour law. Amongst other things, the draft law ratifys two International Labour Organisation conventions on safety and health and the elimination of violence at work. The important directive on a work-life balance is also incorporated. The Ministry of Labour attempts to deal with workers' problems, youth and women's injustices, as well as anachronistic working arrangements, following the storming changes that have taken place in the area of employment in the digital age. The draft law protects eight hours and five days, combats black work and undeclared overtime. The digital working card, which is linked to the information system ‘ERGAN’, is established so that it can be checked at any time if there are excesses in the working hours of each worker. This is, indeed, an innovation in controlling undeclared and undeclared work. There are also rights for male workers, such as paternity leave and protection for those working in new forms of employment, such as teleworking and digital platforms. More fundamental areas of the draft law, which we are discussing in trade union law, are measures for transparency in trade unions, the introduction of civil liability for trade unionists who, while strikeing, prevent those who do not participate in the strike from working, the definition of a minimum percentage - of 33% - guaranteed service in the course of strikes for the common interest, for the protection - obviously - of the social system, as is the case in many European countries. As far as the working hours are concerned, there are no fundamental changes in the issues which concern eight hours, five hours and forty hours a week, in relation to the current system, despite the efforts and excesses - let me say - many to the contrary. Arrangements are being introduced in the framework of a working time arrangement, the safeguarding and raising of overtime in a hundred and fifty hours a year with a parallel equation in industry and other sectors, just as in force in the European Union. The bill also provides for the establishment of an independent labour inspectorate at the post of the Labour Inspectorate with functional independence and financial self-evidence. The purpose of this regulation is to exclude any political interference in the control of the labour market. With regard to the balance of working and family life, positive regulations are introduced in the draft law. There are fourteen days of paternity leave - against ten today - protection of the new father against dismissal for six months from the child's birth, parental leave for the two parents, and for the first time for two months. In order to deal with violence in the draft law, there is an increase in protection and in violence and in simple harassment. As far as teleworking is concerned, which has now entered for good in our lives and has obviously come to stay, it is implemented after an agreement between workers and employers. Teleworkers have the same rights and obligations as workers within the establishment of an undertaking. I have listened with caution to the rapporteurs and many colleagues in opposition. Many of them invoked Law 1264/1982 on 'democracy of the trade union movement', which is, of course, amended by the draft law under discussion. It is right, ladies and gentlemen, that this law is being modernised. It is universal the changes that have taken place in the labour sector - and not just - over the last few decades. In conclusion, the bill attempts an important step forward by adapting it to best European practices, such as strengthening parental leave, overtime, restoring working time, teleworking, modernising labour law, restoring injustices, abolishing anachronistic regulations. I mention, for example, the amendment to a 1920 regulation, ladies and gentlemen, concerning discrimination between employees and workers. At the same time, arrangements are being made for transparency in trade unions and measures which protect both the right to strike, of course, and the social system as a whole from similar abuses. In summary, I would say that the draft law serves the needs of work in the digital organisation of our society. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u142	Mr. Ljupis from New Democracy has the floor, followed by Mr. Kalamataianos from SYRIZA, who also closes the ninth cycle.
ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u143	Thank you, Mr President. Madam Minister, ladies and gentlemen, today we are discussing a draft law which has only been a draft law. The opposition condemned it from the first moment, calling it "anti-democratic" and "anti-democratic". It is, though, isn't it? Let us see only some of the provisions of the bill that are first introduced into the Greek legal order. The new protective framework, which includes a number of permits and favourable provisions, inclusive of all working parents, natural stepfathers, contractors, mothers who have a child, through a parent mother. For the first time, a license is introduced for people, relatives or even people living in the same home, for serious medical reasons. To facilitate workers with children they choose flexible forms of work for a certain period of time, such as teleworking, flexible working hours or parttime work, when children are up to 12 years old. To date, maternity leave was considered to be self-evident, but not paternity leave. Gender equality and the need to create a strong emotional bond between children and their father presupposes active participation of the father from the early hours of children's lives. For the first time, a licence of 14 days and a father is provided for. The measure of non-dismissal is extended to the new father up to six months after the child was born. An important omission is corrected. Parenthood and paternity leave are taken by those who adopt a child up to 8 years of age. The school performance monitoring licence is extended to eighteen years from 16 years until now, and for the first time parents of disabled children who are in special education structures, CEDEFOP or CEDEFOP. This is a very important step towards improving parental leave, distributed at various intervals up to the age of eight for each child. The big change is that for the first time the two of the four months are subsidised by the OAED and therefore a strong incentive is provided for parents to use it. Finally, who can disagree with the fact that the employer's abuses are widening, making dismissal invalid? In fact, I wonder: can a single Member of Parliament be found, who will vote against all these favourable regulations, which support the worker? I now come to the provisions that have caused the strong reaction of counter-population. The permanent incubation these days is that it is affected by the established eighth. This is not just the case, but forty hours of work and five-hour work are repeated in Article 55 clearly, in detail and without room for question. The next popular review is that overtime will not be paid. On the contrary, in fact overtime is increased from a hundred to twenty in a hundred and fifty, which is the European average, and of course, they will be paid 40% more. Let's see what's actually changing. The worker is offered a choice of how he wishes to allocate his working time. The worker can choose four-day to ten hours a day, which is a full-time job in the system of working time adjustment. The settlement is not an innovation in today's bill. It has already existed in Greek labour legislation for three decades. Moreover, on the basis of the European directive that we incorporate, it is perfectly legitimate and flexible working arrangements for parents and sick careers. Another big lie, which has been heard, is that the workers will be paid off the day off. All workers working for more than 40 hours a week will normally be paid for overtime. Nothing changes at this point. It must be understood that all this is done for workers who wish to have one day of the week free to take advantage of it as they wish. No worker can be forced to work four hours a week and no worker can be fired because he refused to work in this way. A single and clear framework for teleworking is being established. Greece is the third country in Europe, which establishes the right to disconnect out-of-time and, of course, at the time of the worker's permission. A great deal of innovation is the digital working card. Each employer must have a computerized working time logging system directly linked to 'ERGAN II'. Thus, they are all recorded in real time, such as the start and termination of work, breaks, and all overtime. Upgrading the EIA to an Independent Authority increases its credibility and objectivity, aiming to concentrate the confidence of workers, who will report more easily violations of labour law. Finally, the changes in trade union legislation have also caused disagreements. But what exactly can one disagree with? With the complete abolition of the financing of trade unions by employers and party organisations? By digitizing the books of the trade union members? With the possibility of everyone, even remote people, being able to participate in the assemblies and in the votes? I do not think that anyone can disagree with the widest possible legalisation achieved. Finally, Mr President, all the measures taken by today's bill are also aimed at strengthening the institution of the family. The facilities and rights guaranteed give encouragement to Greek women and Greeks to have a family, knowing that labour legislation supports them. Greece's demographic problem is a very complex issue, the solution of which lies in many small and larger family interventions. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Civic Opposition uses as a pretext the interests of workers to raise political tones, falsely suggesting that employment rights are affected. No employee's right is offended by the bill. On the contrary! The workers who have been given the initiative for the first time to choose how to manage and distribute their working time are fully guaranteed. This is a truly charitable bill, in my opinion, which goes hand in hand with the demands of the time in terms of the needs of the workers. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u201	Thank you very much, Mr Colleague. Ladies and gentlemen, for weeks now we have been confronted with the labour bill, the government by the mouth of Mr Hatzidakis, by the Prime Minister's mouth, says that by this bill he is legislating for the protection and interests of workers. I think it won't take me more than three, four minutes to prove to you what interests it just legislates and what interests it serves. I would point out, therefore, that the study entitled 'The future of work in a changing world', published in a special report on BSE, says BSE: 'The rapid technological developments and the COVID crisis have shown that the human resources should be flexible and resilience'. The "Hatzidakis" bill comes to make these vague concepts, flexibility and durability specific to the two basic arrangements. One relates to work for more than eight hours without additional pay, a forecast which obviously exhausts the worker, apparently reduces his wages and obviously creates a stimulus for new recruitments since the employer can "manage" the existing worker. However, it is doing the BSE request. The other regulation, which deals with the fact that parttime workers will now have to work more than agreed hours, at waste hours, obviously dismantles the private life of the worker, obviously eliminates him and obviously creates a stimulus for new recruitment. However, it is doing the BSE request. But let's go a little further, 'cause things get more interesting. In a recent article by the Senior Advisor of BSE, in the BSE sector, it speaks of the urgent need to increase the anachronistic limits, as it says, of legal overtime work. Mr Hatzidakis' bill comes to do exactly this, that is to say it increases the limits of legal overtime to 50 hours per annum, which obviously jeopardizes the health of workers, obviously prevents new recruitment and obviously coupled with the adjustment of working time, can also lead to work for up to thirteen hours per day. However, it is doing the BSE request. In a BSE conference on "The future of work after the Memorandum", the BSE Management Officer argues that the problem of Law 1264, the trade union law, is the excessive range of protection for trade unions. So the Hatzidakis bill also comes here and does this desire and reduces the number of trade unions protected from dismissal. In a text of the Association of Thessaly and Central Greece Industries at the same BSE conference, the Association proposes that overtime should be recorded electronically, overtime, overtime changes, but retrospectively. After providing the job, the employer must have a margin of days to change, change, declare otherwise, etc. Obviously, post-retrospective registration does not allow control and obviously leaves room for any form of employers' immunity and employer's arbitrariness. Mr Hatzidakis' bill comes to do just this, that is to say the employer's right to rectify all these changes in the working hours and amend them, that is to say, to act another request from the BSE question. In an article by the Legal Adviser to BSE on labour matters it says in particular: it would be desirable, it says, a regulation in which the trade union organisation which declares a strike will take responsibility for the work of the strikers, and even for the penalty. In other words, it must also have some legal effect on taking up this responsibility. The Hatzidakis bill provides precisely that the trade union organization declaring the strike is responsible for the work of the strikers. In fact, it is foreseen that if it fails to fulfil its responsibility, then the strike will also be void, but the trade union organization will also have to pay compensation. The neoliberal "think tank", the Centre for Liberal Studies (KEFIM), his beloved Mr.Mitsotakis, in a recent study on labour, suggests that, among other things, the employer should be given the opportunity instead of warning the employee about dismissal, to dismiss him immediately, paying more as compensation. Mr Hatzidakis' bill also implements this very demand, namely that the employer be able to get over with the worker very quickly, without any other ones and leave the worker completely undisturbed. It also provides for something even more extreme, that in the event of a dismissal being declared void by the court, the employer will not have the responsibilities and consequences of the nullity, i.e. to pay late payment and re-take him, but will be relieved of these obligations, as if the court had not decided that the dismissal was void! The CPMM also specifically asks - everything I say is in introductory terms what they have required - that the employer does not comply with the standard conditions for dismissal should not automatically lead to the dismissal being invalidated, but be able to make corrections. Mr. Hatzidakis' bill says exactly that. The CCCTB also says that the dismissal of trade unions should be allowed in every case of great reason and not for the current restrictive cases that exist in the law. Mr Hatzidakis' bill also provides for this request, namely that trade unions should be made redundant in every case of great reason and not just for the current restrictive reasons. So I think it is obvious and there is no need for any further debate on who this government is legislating. Legislation on the hard core of BSE. It's self-proof. What's the aim of this piece of legislation? We have nothing to do with a series of countermeasures. We are dealing with a project that is very structured, very coherent. The project is a project of depreciation of work. It is the project of reducing labour costs. On this road, the government is aware of growth. It was thus understood by this party before 2014. So now he's coming to catch the thread from this plan that he was already implementing by the end of 2014, to continue it. [[This is where the ending bell of the speaking time of the honourable Member rings.]] I shall conclude, Mr President, very briefly. What is the main tool for implementing this project? It's the individual contract of employment. It is the individual contract of employment which allows this inequality to prevail in any event in the parts of the employment relationship, i.e. the employer and the employee. We're talking about two inherently unequal parts. Where there is overpower, an empire of the individual contract of employment, this inequality is established. That is why freedom without equality is not meant. That is why this bill does not release the worker - we have also been told by Mr Hatzidakis - but enslaves him through the pan-state of the individual labour contract. So today, SYRIZA, the trade unions, the entire Opposition to fight, actually to fight this pre-tax status of the individual labour contract, while the needs of the world of work are completely elsewhere. His needs are today in the presence of much greater protection and much more protectionist regulations after what happened with the absolute flexibility that prevailed in COVID. Its needs are to have arrangements for contract workers, to have arrangements to put workers on the platforms under the payroll protection framework, to have more collective agreements covering more workers. Needs lie in moving towards thirty-five hours, without a reduction in wages, creating more full-time jobs and not unleashing existing workers through a tank of cheap overtime and increasing the purchasing power of wages. This is where the needs of work are and not what this bill contains. Thank you very much.

Neither are we abolishing, nor are we establishing unpaid overtime, nor have we declared war on the world of work. Instead, we are creating a bill, a protective framework for workers, an institutional framework that is friendly to attract new investments. A key point of criticism for the bill is the adjustment of working time. The organization of working time does not remove eight hours or replace overtime. It is an alternative to the worker, which has been valid in Greece since 1990. With the change to be made by the worker solely on his own initiative and initiative, he will be able to propose and regulate his working time. Let's see what's going on today. The day-to-day arrangement is, therefore, determined by either business collective agreements, either by agreement between the employer and the trade union or by agreement between the employer and the association of persons. If more than twenty employees work in one enterprise, the association of persons should be at least 25%, if they are less than twenty people, 15% should be involved in the association, i.e. about three people if they are twenty. So let us go to the provisions of the much-discussed draft law. Firstly, the anti-violence measures and the harassment at work. Greece immediately ratifys the Convention 190 of the International Labour Organisation and move on to the next step, introducing a series of new measures and regulations which extend the existing protection and set a comprehensive and modern framework to prevent, combat and deal with behaviours of violence and harassment at work. Secondly, the safety and health measures at work. In this context Greece ratifys the Convention 187 of the International Labour Organisation to promote safety and health at work and we also bring a number of regulations such as the National Health and Safety Programme and the establishment of cooperation between the Ministry of Labour, Labour Inspectorate and the Greek Institute for Health and Safety of Work. Thirdly, the modernisation and upgrading of the Labour Inspectorate, which becomes an Independent Authority. This puts an end to the possibility of possible political intervention. Thus, the Labour Inspectorate leaves control of the respective Minister. It operates independently, transparently and solely in the interests of the worker. Will you also vote against these provisions, ladies and gentlemen of SYRIZA? May I remind you that the Independent Public Revenue Authority was established by Law No.4389/2016 and started its operation on 1-1-2017? Fourthly, new leave for both parents, extension of parental leave and adoption, seven days' leave for assisted reproduction, and in the private sector, is all set up for the first time and are important arrangements for young parents. They contribute greatly to the balance between family and working life. However, on this point, ladies and gentlemen, allow me to table a proposal with which all the groups will agree. Because yesterday was the World Volunteer Day, the proposal is the donation of the donor and those who participate in the blood banks and trade unions from two days to three days. I table this as a proposal, because this bill includes specific and very serious permits and is something very important when our country has to increase volunteering, particularly as regards volunteering for blood donations. And I come to the chapter on trade unions, which is probably the real reason for the reactions. The current regulations introduce transparency in trade unions, trade unions. It ends the irrational privileges, but also the suffering of society. The bill continues to protect trade unions, because this is how it is to be done in all modern states, but some specific institutionalised limits also come in. Votes may also be electronically and from a distance. An obligation is laid down to have guaranteed service staff for critical sectors and services. At the same time, we are also bringing arrangements for further protection of trade and we are extending this right, for example to workers in the collaborative digital platforms. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] It is a lot of provisions and they are all just as important. ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u243	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, we are discussing today at the National Representation a draft law on the protection of workers, which in essence regulates and effectively tackles, modern and real workers' problems and lays new foundations in the labour structure. It is undeniable that in a period of global economic crisis in the midst of the pandemic, balances in the workplace have changed. Labour rights have been affected and are generally discriminated against and inconsistent with the rights of workers. So let's see what this bill does. It includes a series of arrangements that restore chronic injustices. It abolishes or revises anachronistic regulations, improves a number of backward practices and adapts the framework of labour legislation to best European practices. The truth is that many times we have to look at the real value of the proposed arrangements. We must understand somewhere that the sterile opposition that is being made without specific counter-proposals only to satisfy a particular party does not bring any positive results and does not mobilise our potential for change, which is what is being called. So let us leave any micro-political and micro-participatory purposes. This bill is a marriage to growth and competitiveness, mainly because it hits black and implied work using the new digital card with the ultimate aim of combating tax evasion and avoiding all kinds of work that is not paid. It will be fully monitored and recorded in the hours of arrival and departure of the worker, his break time, the overrun of legal working hours, the licenses and overtime, all on a digital card, without the employer having the ability to change the components of this card at the appropriate time. It is therefore a useful tool in the worker's hands to safeguard his rights, while helping to reduce bureaucracy. In short, it will be the guarantee that its rights in matters of time, payment and remuneration will be respected. Measures are also adopted in the current draft law to prevent, deal with and combat behaviours of violence and harassment in the public and private sectors. Measures are promoted to ban all forms of violence or harassment in any way. The obligations of the employer relating to prevention and treatment are specified, while the rights of the affected are now regulated, as are the consequences of any such infringement. A decisive measure provided for is the complete prohibition and nullity of the complaint or any way of resolving the legal relationship that acts as a countermeasure to a complaint, testimony or provision of information on an incident of violence or harassment at the workplace. The worker, in other words, will not be afraid to denounce any form of harassment at work at the risk of redundancies. We are one of the first countries to make such arrangements. A separate new independent administrative authority of the Labour Inspectorate is also set up to monitor and implement labour legislation. This is a principle that functions directly and effectively in dealing with any infringements detected in the workplace. It will exercise its powers as an independent and independent body, without being inclined to control the Ministry of Labour. The top priority of today's bill is, of course, the interdependent and harmonious link between working and family life. In what way? The paternity leave, as the previous speakers have said, is being promoted for fourteen days compared with two to date, while the new father is being protected against dismissal for six months. Can anyone seriously argue that it is not particularly important and, above all, humanitarian? Is it not particularly important that the protection of pregnant and working mothers is strengthened and that new flexible rules are promoted for the worker, such as teleworking? These are, therefore, the arrangements by which it is effectively protected and shielded and, in practice, the institution of the family. The broad use of teleworking, as I said before, combined with the acceleration of digital transformation and the strengthening of the digital capabilities of citizens and businesses is directly linked to further productivity growth. This digital transformation of the public sector is a key factor in our country's development. All this should be self-evident for all of us. For all these, of course, who want to improve the productivity and competitiveness of our businesses. So the bill puts the employee as a priority, as it includes a number of regulations that support him directly. The worker must be treated as an equal member. Fixed requests, such as the abolition of discrimination between employees and workers, are met, while their allowances, which were few and poor, are equal to those of officials. We are therefore, in practice, breaking this vicious cycle of inequality in the workplace and adopting measures to support the weak. Your claim, gentlemen, to abolish the eighth is, of course, a funny genius and opposition trick. The eighth was, it is, and will be, regulated, as from now on not only will it be foreseen, but it will be implemented. We are able to ask the employer to work more for a period of time, gaining leave at a time when he chooses, is something that applies abroad and we cannot distort his essence and deprive people of it. In addition, in this draft law, the New Democracy Government puts order and takes measures to ensure transparency in trade unionism. All trade unions will be registered electronically and in detail. The registration of the statutes, members, seat and purpose is necessary. This will strengthen the credibility and representativeness of the trade union movement. The new draft law protects trade unions from redundancies and allows only for a great reason, which the courts will judge. Contrary to what is supported these days by the Opposition falsely, the right to strike is guaranteed, as it is a constitutionally guaranteed right, but also self-evidently guaranteed in this bill. Ladies and gentlemen, Greece is taking a big step forward. It follows the policies of the most advanced European countries. It resists voices that systematically deny changes and reforms. It moves forward together, implementing policies for the benefit of citizens by promoting growth, innovation and competitiveness. So against all those who systematically unleash charges without an alternative proposal, the Government of New Democracy responds with works, responds by means of actions. The first, misinformation - I said before - that overtime will be compensated for by the day off, that it will be abolished in the evening. For a time, and the Civic Anti-Civilization underwent this spermology and adopted it in the context of the attempt to form a political reason, which obviously has no. And it makes no provision for any political power to have to resort to falsehood to opposition and I am sure - I do not stumble - that many SYRIZA colleagues do not feel comfortable with these practices. But anyway, the truth is revealed at the end and it sets out those who are trying to overthrow it and distort it, so it makes no sense for me to remain in this fast-news campaign, which we have seen again and I am sure, unfortunately, that we will see from the standpoint of the Officerial Response, as well as the broader parliamentary representation of the Greek Left. On the other hand, it is very useful to respond with documentation and empathy to the second kind of criticism because it comes, Madam Minister, to the core of our liberal democracy. And what's that? It starts from finding an asymmetry. Employers support those who express - the JRC often says and has a right to it - this criticism is inherently and legally in a position of power vis-à-vis workers. Given this reality, therefore, says this criticism, any policy that strengthens choices strengthens asymmetrically the strong employers against the weak workers. This is also supported by the expressions of this criticism, not unfounded, in practice. Let's see, for example, the reality of black and undeclared work, see the introductory "testing" periods of work that never end up intake, see the gifts paid by the bad employer, the bad employer, and the other demand. So in the face of this reality of exploiting who really believes that greater flexibility in work will indeed be in favour of workers? This is where we need to speak politically, namely with arguments and examples. And the truth is that 80% -90% of the real problems in the labour pool are solved, we liberals at least believe, when the economy is healthy, is strong and growth is sustainable, that is, when there are many good jobs. Why? Because a bad employer will take advantage of you when he knows you have no choice. However, when a worker can at any time claim better working conditions to a competitor, the margins are dramatically narrowed. I said before that the healthy, strong, dynamic economy solves 80% with 90% of the occupational pathogens. But there is a 10% to 20% where strict, undefeated, certain law enforcement is needed. In other words, there is a need for rules of general application, inspection bodies which are not accessible to political influences, which will be accountable to society in terms of the results of their action and which their executives will not be able to apply or apply the laws. There are two conditions, therefore: a healthy economy and a fair and modern regulatory framework which is observable. This is the double targeting of this bill. On the one hand, it modernises the regulatory environment, strengthens the freedom of choice for workers and employers, puts in new technological possibilities that we have seen working in the pandemic and learns lessons from it. On the other hand, however, it strengthens the protection of workers and creates new ones: The digital working card. The creation of the Independent Labour Inspectorate. The new possibilities for settling working time are very important. The introduction of new permits for parents and caregivers. Ensuring the right of disconnection, fundamental in the new era and in the third millennium. The new framework for protection against harassment - here it is - at the workplace. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I shall conclude, Madam President. All of this shows that the Government of New Democracy and the Lord Mitsotakis does not just legislate to legislate or "close the eye" to someone else, but responsibly and seriously serves the common good. I shall end in a minute, Madam President, because I know that, as you must, you will make a recommendation to me. So this commitment to the common good should be safeguarded. The second, most serious, criticism of this bill does not question the correct logic of the bill, but the State's ability to implement what the State has adopted. That's why we have to be clear. This bill will be judged in practice when some go to violate their provisions with'so I want'. It will be the first time that an employer has undertaken not to pay the wage due for work. It will be judged when an employer tells a worker that "you will take them black and undeclared". Or he will be judged when he says: 'Get back the Christmas present.' Or it will be judged when a working father attempts to close with "so I want" an operation without the strike being declared lawful, as happened. It will be judged the next time we have the same scenes in the port, when illegally and abusively there is a problem and the state is looking at it. It will be judged the first time that chief trade unionists - let us not forget these - and an employer hand - will try to find them under the table with donations for conferences and holidays.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u347	Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law before us comes after nearly half a century to reform, modernise and, above all, harmonize Greek labour legislation with that of the previous European states. This bill comes to meet the new needs of today, the modern digital reality and the labour market of the twenty-first century. The Law of the Ministry of Labour is another reform initiative by the Government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis for the benefit of Greek society. However, the Opposition and its extreme ideological conservation urge us to withdraw the bill that is currently being voted on, listing such designations as "structural", "laboratory anomalies", "laboratory middle ages". And I ask you, ladies and gentlemen of the Opposition, do you condemn a bill which in two-thirds concerns the ratification of international conventions and the incorporation of a European directive? I have heard Mrs Xinoyannakopoulou say that we are using international conventions as a front. Listen, listen! SYRIZA colleagues view our international obligations and our compliance with them as a front! You demand the ratification of a most important convention, such as the Convention 190 of the International Labour Organisation to eliminate violence and harassment at work. The Government of New Democracy, on the other hand, ratifys the 190 Convention and, at a time enough earlier than that which the directive itself lays down as a deadline for its incorporation in August 2022, because the Government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis devours the needs of society and displays extremely reflexively in the 'Me Too' movement and in the public complaints of all victims of powerless violence in their workplace. At the same time, with this draft law, we are dealing with time-consuming and unresolved problems of working parents. We are introducing new parental leave, such as a 14-day paternity leave with acceptance, and for the first time we are guaranteeing the protection of a new father against dismissal for six months. On the basis of gender equality, the new regulation also restricts women's inclination. Protection against redundancies is not only extended to young fathers, but is expanded much more and extended in any case, but also to the pursuit of any right on the part of a worker. After so many years, we turn the case law of the courts into law. And now we're going to lies, inaccurate, parafiscality, and dangerous ravines about the infamous eight-hour abolition. Is the eighth abolished? Clearly not. Not only will it not be abolished, but it will also be applied substantially with the new digital working card that we import. In fact, the eighth hour is guaranteed in all the countries of the European Union and is not meant to be abolished in any Member State. Colleagues of counter-population should be aware of this, rather than engaged in a fight for disinformation and disorientation of society. So what exactly is it with the time arrangement? The resolution of working time is a European institution which has been in place for several decades both in Europe and in Greece. It does not increase the total working time of the worker, as is the case with overtime, but it enables the worker to concentrate their work in fewer days. He may, in other words, adjust his work at the request of himself according to his or her personal or family needs and obligations. In this way, for example, he will be able to work a little longer, Monday to Thursday and Friday not to work, but to get paid and see more his family or study, for example, to attend a postgraduate, keeping in any case the inviolability of a maximum of five days or forty hours a week. Is this the flexibility of anti-gaut regulation? Of course it isn't. Is it happening today? It's happening. Did it happen in your days? It happened in many large businesses. So what do you want? Do you want to be able to organise and facilitate their lives only those who work in large trade unions and the other thousands of workers working in small or medium-sized enterprises who do not have trade unions do not have the same chance? We say the same right for all workers to have it, regardless of their trade union activities. Now we're going to the overtime institution. In this case, and only in this case is the worker working over 40 hours a week, at the same time collecting more money. With this bill, the overtime envisaged is one hundred and fifty hours a year, just as the average European term is. In return for limiting time due to overtime, there is a higher pay rate, because overtime will be paid 40% more, yes, with more money, not with a little off-take as some are falsely spreading. In addition, by digital recording of working time overtime overtime will be checked and paid out as a whole. And I'm wondering again, is the digital card an antique measure? Obviously not. The digital working card is a pioneering and modern tool that will ensure accurate recording and compensation for all overtime workers. Plus, the other ones are our black-work gun. At the same time, a powerful weapon to all violations of labour law is the independent authority that we recommend, the Labour Inspectorate. The Labour Inspectorate will enjoy functional independence, administrative and financial autonomy and will not be subject to governmental control or supervision. It will operate in the standards of the AIA, with all guarantees for independent and effective operation. I close, Madam President, by collective labour law. You have accused us of slowing down trade union freedom when we are strengthening the transparency of trade unions by providing for an electronic voting register and a ban on their aid from employers and parties, precisely to strengthen the free and fair functioning of trade unions. In closing, faithful, ladies and gentlemen, at the beginning, our programme and our election commitments, we are making progress on all key reforms. We create Greece that we all dream of, the Greece that we deserve. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u405	Thank you, Mrs Rapt, especially for keeping time. Mr. George Papaliou has the floor from SYRIZA. ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u406	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the content of the bill is linked to the question of how to regulate labour relations that has been set up for many years, it has then been answered and the bill is answered in a concrete way. I'll explain how. Labour law is a conquest of the world of work. At its core lies the contract of employment. Initially it was one of the replacement contracts, like all others, in the context of freedom of trade. The contract of employment was seconded from other civil law replacement contracts, because its object, the provision of work, is interconnected with man himself and cannot be separated from him. In providing work, the worker's labour force comes and is identified as the worker himself who is a man of flesh and bone, but also of personality. Moreover, the worker as the weak side of the work relationship does not negotiate on equal terms with the employer and therefore needs protection. Thus, labour law was born, as autonomous law in relation to civil law, annoying law, gaining and having a significant protectionist dimension. And in its evolution, labour law acquired another extremely important dominant dimension, the collective dimension. Negotiations are carried from individual to collective level so that workers can negotiate with the employer collectively through the trade unions and thus achieve an elementary balance, given the inherent imbalance of power between employers and workers. The neoliberal policies that prevailed mainly in the last 20th century and at the beginning of the 21st century on the basis of the association of forces, social and political policies that have emerged, have attempted and attempted to annul the collective dimension of Labour Law by dismantling the trade union movement, the deterioration of collective labour agreements, in short with complete dismantling, the deregulation of collective labour law. The individual contract of employment thus returns as a dominant factor in the form of and regulates the terms of work and hence the inequality of the two parties in the employment relationship. This is also noted in the report by the Scientific Committee of the Parliament. In this respect, i.e. the incorporation of individual contracts of employment into a dominant shaper of the work relationship, the current bill is also under way. This is a fundamental element of the New Democracy policy. It moves backwards and not towards progress, which, ladies and gentlemen of the New Democracy, you constantly call on, since regression is not necessarily linked to the time of introduction, to the time for the adoption of protective labour regulations. This is in your view that, due to the fact that labour rights have been introduced over the years, they must be adapted to the current 'working jungle', i.e. abolished. That's what you're saying. And let us not forget that the Prime Minister himself has described the eight-hour anachronism and ideological obsession, while, Minister and fellow Members, progress is being made with the employers' arbitrage and the "working jungle" you are establishing. Moreover, this is your argument, namely that since there is a 'working jungle' on the market, as realists you institutionalize and therefore legalize it. So the work reality ceases to be illegal, and it becomes legal, with the bogeyman and your bill. Gentlemen of the New Democracy, that's your policy. And even by strengthening the memorandum policies, whose labour law and the position of the world of work have been their main victim, and indeed the provisional Memorandum measures have become permanent, i.e. the crime committed is not instant but lasting, and this is after the country's exit from the memoranda - to remember this, in August 2018 - that is, without any commitments. In addition, after taking over the country's governance, the New Democracy has promoted arrangements that have overturned those established under SYRIZA's rule after the exit of the country from the memoranda: the non-promotion of further increase in lower wages, the abolition of the justified nature of redundancies and co-responsibility, a set of exceptions to the scope of collective labour agreements, the mutilation of the right to unilateral recourse to arbitration, the failure to subject PPC's new entrants and ELTA to the arrangements of business collective labour agreements and labour regulations etc. Thus the Government of New Democracy, with extreme neoliberal perceptions, restores the negotiation of working conditions at individual level by strengthening this transfer and by institutional cancellation - undermining collective autonomy, trade union action and the right to strike. And this despite the pandemic that has even led to liberal and conservative governments revise in part some of their extreme neoliberal views and bring back to sensitive areas such as work and health, state and collective intervention, so that at least in these fields it is not all left to market arbitrary. Ladies and gentlemen, the current bill is a false document, because another is its aim - I have developed it before - and another is its title. Signed 'to protect the work'. Of course you are canceling the protection of the weak side of the contract of employment. But you are also doing one - I would say - political dishonesty. Incorporate into the bill the ratification of two international conventions and the incorporation of a European directive. In this way you are attempting to cover up the anti-social, anti-democratic and authoritarian character of the bill that will result in the institutional enforcement of the "working jungle".

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u493	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I cannot go into the temptation because we are not yesterday. I entered politics in 2007, and I remember the geography of people in the 2009 elections very well. After a few years, I remember SYRIZA's conferences and conferences. One was the voice: "PASOK is here united, strong." I'm sorry, but that's the truth. I now enter this very serious bill, which I consider to be an incision to our country's labour data. It is a pity that the opposition, whether gainful or not, did not take this opportunity productively to contribute to a debate in order to improve the law's provisions and provide citizens with positive services. Unfortunately, it was followed by an extreme opposition tactic, I would say, in a micro-political sense, a "no" in everything, describing the bill as supposedly anti-government and calling with incredible names this law, "excitement, roller, retractor" and much more. But what is the truth? Reading the more than a hundred twenty articles of this law, we see that it is primarily a charitable law. It does protect work, it actually ratifys serious conventions by the International Labour Organisation and of course incorporates into the existing European directive on the balance between work and family life. This directive allows for the right to vote for benefits which have so far not existed, which helps the motherhood, fatherhood and strengthens the family. This is very important because nowadays, unfortunately, ladies and gentlemen, the major problem facing the country is demographic. Therefore, when there are such laws that help the family, only positive can they be. There is one argument from the Opposition that these positive provisions are a contour, and that means are anti-corruption provisions and extremities against the workers of provision. But that's not the way it is. I think it's clear. You are shouting from the opposition that it is lifting the eighth hour. But how is the eighth to be abolished, since Article 55 precisely guarantees the eighth? You're shouting about the increase in overtime. But why are you shouting at the increase in overtime when the hundred and fifty hours provided for in the law adopt exactly the European average and even an increased fee? There is a lot of talk of adjusting working hours. But the government of Mitsotakis does not find the wheel. The working time arrangement has been in place for thirty years. It is a European tactic that has been under way for many years now. But this law also puts a brake - to allow me to speak - on trade union immunity. Many things all these years had taken a bad path. I will say a few words about this: the possibility of financing trade unions and operators by businesses, parties and by ECHO is being abolished. This ensures their independence. Finally, it is very important, and this is very important, to protect the social fabric from the consequences of the strikes, since it is envisaged to ensure that the minimum guaranteed service is provided for the common interest defined in 1/3 and that is very essential. At the same time, it puts an end to the phenomenon of the circumvention of judgments in which strikes judged illegal are re-proclaimed the next day. However, the 'weapon' offered by this law to workers for their protection is also very important. What's that? It is the digital card of the worker who is now electronically talking about everything we have said before - that is, overtime, work arrangement, eight hours or if you want to work enhanced - it seems that the worker is being protected, because there cannot be immunity from employers. This, together with the introduction for the first time of teleworking, creates a new landscape that actually serves the worker and sets a framework within which the employer must move. This law is therefore a law that modernises our labour legislation which has remained about the same forty years, helps motherhood, helps fatherhood, helps new families, has too many provisions that restrict bad past phenomena. For all these reasons, it is a reform law that we should all support. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u517	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I shall take the floor to refer to the content of the amendment tabled by the Ministry of Finance to the draft law of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs. This bill comes to address and regulate major issues at work by introducing an innovative institutional framework when the current institutional framework has remained unchanged since 1982. This new institutional framework aims to strengthen social cohesion and strengthen productivity by attracting investment. Ladies and gentlemen, as I said at the beginning of my speech, I have taken the floor to refer to Amendment No 931 and to specific number 64 tabled by the Ministry of Finance and deals with issues relating to tax policy. These are regulations aimed at supporting those affected by the pandemic, and also to modernise and simplify the current regulatory framework. Article 1 updates the first category of vessel tax and levy rates of Law 27/1975 and the first category of contribution of Law 29/1975. In particular, the total tonnage and age amounts of the ship are increased for the years 2021 to 2025 by 4% annually, and the same applies to the amounts of the contribution. The aim is to increase the revenue from the adjustment of the tax rates and the first-class ship contribution under Law 27/1975, as well as the rates of contribution under Law 29/1975 for the five-year period 2021-2025, while suspending the increase especially for 2021 due to the apparent and visible consequences of the pandemic. Article 2 updates and rationalises the rates of taxation for small private pleasure boats. To date, private pleasure boats, regardless of size, had an increase rate of 5 on the calculated tax per vessel. By means of the arrangements to be voted on, vessels up to seven metres long will have an increase rate of 0,5 and vessels from seven to twelve metres will have a coefficient of 1. Article 3 clarifies the terms, conditions and procedure for the payment of the proposed grant for the transport costs of publishing undertakings. It is clear that this grant is inappropriate, with the exception of debts to tax administration and non-regulated insurance funds. With a view to streamlining procedures and facilitating taxpayers’ proceedings, the next article shall be regulated by which the obligation to produce evidence of tax information on behalf of the person who is to seize in the event of a payment of a payment order by the State, when such payment is made in a final judicial decision, is abolished. Article 5 seeks to strengthen the liquidity of undertakings active in the oil market, but without at the same time cause for delay for annual public revenue. The facility provided is a small credit up to five days for the period up to 20 December each year to the oil companies for payment to customs of charges for liquid fuels and liquefied gas leaving tax warehouses. I note that the credit is granted under guarantee, thereby ensuring 100% of the amount corresponding to taxes. As I have said many times in my position in Parliament, the government will constantly update or renew support measures for businesses, workers and social groups affected by the negative effects of the pandemic. This is the direction in which Article 6 of the amendment is extended and for June, which provides for a full exemption from the obligation to pay a rent for enterprises active in the tourism, culture, sport, transport and catering sectors, where restrictive measures are imposed. At the same time, it should be stressed that the payment of a reduced rental of 40% is in force in June for the retail trade, for the main residence of workers who are suspended in June and for the student residence of these dependent members. [[At this point, the closing bell of the speaking time of the Secretary-General is ringing.]] I'm gonna need two more minutes, Mr. President. The aim of the Government at all times is to proceed to institutional interventions on the basis of rational criteria, which facilitate citizens. A certain difficulty has been recorded in registering property in the real estate register, due to the fact that there is a large number of irrevocable pre-agreements due to the incompatibility of the seller of his heirs. The regulation to be voted on allows definitive contracts to be drawn up in a pre-convention with the right to self-convention and without requiring the presentation of evidence of information, certificate of inheritance tax, donation and parental benefit. Under Article 8, the Government extends, for 2021, the exemption of the agricultural parcels of natural persons from the supplementary ENPI, while maintaining for 2021 the interim reduction of 22% of HPAI for natural persons, a reduction made by the New Democracy Government for the first time with Prime Minister Kyriakos Mitsotakis for the first time and resulting in the issue of liquidating HPAI for the first time a reduced tax. ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u539	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill that we are discussing today gives the worker the freedom to establish with his employer his time and working conditions. It strengthens the supervisory bodies of employers themselves and upgrades the Labour Inspectorate as an independent administrative authority. It ratifys two very important contracts of work, Convention 187 of the International Labour Organisation to promote safety and health at work and Convention 190 to eliminate violence and harassment in the world of work. It further harmonizes Greek Labour Law with European Union rules. This introduces a broad and substantial ban on misuse of power as all forms of violence and harassment manifested during work are banned, whether linked to it or resulting from it, including violence and sexual harassment due to sex and sexual harassment. New procedures shall be introduced according to the size of the undertaking to examine these complaints in such a way as to ensure the protection of the victim and respect for human dignity. It is also recommended in the Labour Inspectorate that a separate section be set up to monitor the effects of violence and harassment at work and to keep an employer register against which an administrative penalty has been imposed for violating the ban on violence and harassment at work. The equality bill it introduces for enterprises takes a step further by contributing to the elimination of discrimination on grounds of sex and by strengthening employers' behaviour which respects the equality of workers. It creates an even stronger foundation, a healthy and fair environment for women who, even today, have not been able to enjoy equal conditions on the labour market, despite the fact that we are not only as capable but also sometimes having a higher level of education and qualifications. Nevertheless, we still enjoy wages below our qualifications and less opportunities to develop in our careers. The wager of women's balanced participation in management positions in particular in enterprises is not only a woman's own bet but a social and economic bet. In a financial performance study carried out in enterprises from all over the Greek economy for 2019, women in management positions exceed 10 key financial indicators: in indicators of profitability, efficiency indicators and capital structure indicators. It is therefore a bet that we need to win both for ourselves and for our businesses and our national economy in particular. And this bill helps us to that end. Furthermore, the draft law provides for the proper application of the health and safety measures of workers and strengthens the institution of the occupational physician. The doctrine which the Government of New Democracy has demonstrated and which, in practice, serves faithfully above all health, now also applies in the workplace. Very important is the incorporation of the European Union Directive 2019/1158 on the balance between professional and private life for parents and caregivers. The bill lays down minimum requirements to achieve the predicted equality between men and women in terms of opportunities in the labour market and in the workplace, even by facilitating the combination of working and family life for parents. The right to paternity leave of fourteen days shall be regulated, which shall be obtained at the birth of the child. The legislator takes into account the needs of the modern father to be also close to his children and to enjoy paternity, especially in the precious days along with his newborn baby or new child in the family, in the case of having a child with adoption. It is introduced for both parents a four-month parental leave to be taken until the child closes his eight years and additional child-care leave is also entitled, as well as reduced working hours for the parents of young children, as well as for children with disabilities and other family-related leave. So both parents will be able to contribute substantially to raising their children and respond both to their children’s practices and mental needs. There is provision for single parent families as well. Very important is the article regulating flexible working conditions. According to this, every child worker aged up to 12 years or caregiver is entitled to request flexible work arrangements for care purposes, such as teleworking, flexible working hours or part-time. The employer must expressly justify the rejection of the request or any deferral of flexible working arrangements to the parents and caregivers. There are no more friendly arrangements than these. Finally, forty hours a week are even more guaranteed by the digital working card. The choice of a free day facilitates the professional and family life of workers and is fully compatible with the provisions in force, collective agreements and arbitration decisions. And teleworking has now been seen in practice how useful it can be either pandemic or hopefully in normal conditions, and it has indeed been time to institutionalise. This is a bill that meets the current needs of the workers and shields their rights, improves their working conditions and quality of life and deserves to be voted in favour of and adopted in the interests of all. Thank you very much.

[[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] Minister, ladies and gentlemen, while following all these days in both the Standing Committee on Social Affairs and in plenary the debate on the draft law on the protection of work, I do not hide from you that I am once again fully disappointed by the behaviour and positions of counter-population.
