One would expect at least more serious and responsible behaviour from the Vice-Presidents and, in particular, the Officerial Representation in the parliamentary process of voting on the institutional arrangements necessary for the modernisation and progress of the country and its citizens. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill we have been debating these days is a necessary instrument for modernising and updating labour legislation, which has been in place since the 1980s and some of its chapters since the 1960s and 1920s. It aims, on the one hand, at Greece's production and prosperity, which is attractive to new investment in reducing unemployment by the necessary regulations in labour law, taking into account modern economic and technological developments and, on the other, on strengthening social cohesion by protecting labour, restoring injustices and dealing with problems of today's social reality. It integrates the Convention 187 of the International Labour Organisation for Health and Safety at Work and the good practices of developed countries of the European Union. It establishes the digital working card to combat undeclared overtime work and tax evasion. It upgrades the Labour Inspectorate to an independent authority, excluding possible political interventions in labour market control. It introduces a series of new permits which contribute to equal treatment for men and women. It introduces anti-violence and harassment measures at work with the incorporation of the Convention 190 of the International Labour Organisation. It introduces a framework for the protection of trade union rights for workers on digital platforms, which acquire rights comparable to workers under a contract of conditional employment. It equates the remuneration of workers to those of employees due to dismissal. It regulates teleworking following an agreement between employers and employees. It increases the threshold for permitted overtime on the European average. It introduces flexible working hours. It introduces as an alternative and the adjustment of working time, an institution that has been in place throughout Europe for thirty years. It shall fully reserve the eighth hour of work, in accordance with Article 55. It expands the list of cases of invalid dismissal. It opens up the labour market on Sundays at an additional 75% fee in logistics, data center, nursing material and pharmaceutical industries. It provides for the digital transformation of the labour market monitoring system ‘ERGAN II’ or ECA of Labour Inspectorate, OAED for the necessary cross-checks. It modernises the framework for the functioning of trade unions and trade unions. It equates trade union protection to that of pregnant women. It establishes civil liability for the trade unions who practice violence and act illegally during the strikes. It prohibits the reopening of a strike judged illegal by the judiciary by the secondary and tertiary trade union organisation and which suppresses the place. It protects the community as a whole from strike mobilisations in common advantage through the obligation to provide a guaranteed service to one third of the usual. It introduces a series of measures on transparency into trade unionism. It establishes a ban on the financial support of trade unions by employers and parties, ensuring transparency in their operation. Ladies and gentlemen, these arrangements which, in our view, equate our country with the most developed countries in Europe and the world, are rampant, especially the Civilian Opposition. This is a counter-population below the circumstances, which cries out as a crowd of fields and deplores the government that it abolishes eight hours in a country of the core of the European Union that is in force. With false dilemmas, after she has not been exploited by the victims of the COVID -19 pandemic, she is trying to create a turmoil, protecting extreme minorities who keep the country captive in regression and underdevelopment. The worst thing, however, is that it systematically underestimates the intelligence of the Greek citizens, who have already overrun it and gradually put it where it deserves it, in the calamity of the unusable. Ladies and gentlemen, we New Democracy Members, loyal to our commitments and to our programme adopted by the citizens by a overwhelming majority on 7 July 2019, support the government and the Prime Minister of the country Kyriakos Mitsotakis. We are moving forward with determination and realism in the necessary institutional modernisation of the country that ensures growth and prosperity for all citizens. Society and the majority of citizens are with us and expect us to have faster and measurable results. Finally, I declare that I am voting for the bill under discussion. Thank you, Mr President.

If you don't think it's right, don't vote. The Labour Inspectorate is upgraded to an independent authority in such a way that labour market controls are not influenced by the government's will. We are also introducing measures against violence and harassment at the workplace. In particular, the scope of banned behaviour and protected persons is widened, the obligations of employers, including through the obligation to develop policies on violence and sexual harassment and initiatives to raise public awareness. Existing health and safety measures at the workplace shall be strengthened. The right to disconnect to telework is guaranteed and the cost of equipment, maintenance, etc. is borne by the employer. There is a consistent request from the trade unions to equate workers' allowances to that of employees. With regard to the issues of family and working life reconciliation, a maternity leave of fourteen days shall be introduced with remuneration. A four-month parental leave is provided for each parent, with an OAED grant for the two months. Nine weeks after birth and in the case of the adoption of children. A special leave is granted for the protection of the maternity of six months with a grant by the OAED and for the adoption of children. Reduced working hours, childcare leave, and the mother who acquired a child under the parental maternity procedure are provided. A privately assisted breeding licence shall be granted. A five-day caregiver license is introduced for relatives with care. Flexible working arrangements for parents of children up to 12 years old shall be extended. Isn't that all you like? We establish minimum guaranteed service during strikes in utilities, in principle at 33%. So it will not be possible for a strike to leave the country without power, transportation, collection of waste that poses a natural and danger to public health. However, since there was a lot of talk of the abolition not of the eighth hour, the government included a provision stating that the eight-hour day, weekend and five-day work were not affected. In fact, the bill gives an additional opportunity to implement a four-day weekly employment. The number of hours allowed, overtime, is increased to 50 hours per annum for all enterprises. We are widening the list of cases of invalid dismissal: firstly, redundancies in cases of violence and harassment are invalid. Secondly, the dismissal of the father of a newborn child up to six months old. Thirdly, workers who have received or requested any leave. Fourthly, workers who refused to agree on a set of working hours. Five, teleworkers who exercised the right to disconnect. Finally, we reverse the burden of proof in the dismissal of a worker. It is considered that, in principle, the worker is right and is the employer who must prove otherwise. But there is also the question of financial compensation, instead of re-entry. Ladies and gentlemen, we are before the gates of a huge opportunity, unique for Greece, for the Recovery Fund. It is an opportunity of historical dimensions that is rarely offered in the history of small states and can open new horizons for the Greek economy. Our aim is, on the one hand, to create more and better jobs and, on the other, to support the recovery and competitiveness of the Greek economy. The policies that we are promoting in the field of labour are complementarity, efficiency, strengthening new employment sectors, targeted actions, upgrading the workforce, creating a safety net for all. We're planning an investment cosmopolitan with the aim of covering the investment gap of the last decade. Instead, dear friends of counter-population to meddling and looking for problems where they do not exist, go over and support institutional arrangements, mutually benefit both workers and employers. ParlaMint-GR_2021-06-15-S1-commons.u640	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, this draft law is a modern European bill for adapting our legislation to technological developments and social requirements of our time on the basis of what is happening in the most advanced countries of the European Union. It is a serious attempt to deal with known labour market problems, such as black labour, unpaid overtime, opacity objections to trade unionism and violence. At the same time, it is a bill that introduces modern European practices, strengthens the competitiveness of the Greek economy, but also the balance of professional and family life with regulations such as the adjustment of work, the paternity leave of 14 days and the parental leave of four months and years. Besides, let us not forget that much of this is about incorporating European Directive 2019/1158 which was signed by SYRIZA in June 2019 shortly before the elections. And I mention this in order to highlight the scale of SYRIZA's hypocrisy, which, once again, is using the technique of terrorizing workers by the appropriate perversion process and the well-known propagandaist phrase of the type "drinks the five-day labour, devastates, returns to the middle ages and feudalism" and the other ones well known have grabbed the opportunity to come out of obscurity for so long. However, the provisions under discussion that are supposed to be abolishing the eighth hour are nothing more than the so-called arrangement of working time that Greek legislation has previously recognised and which, since the 1990s, has found full regulatory imprint in the working time directives of the European Union which Greece should apply, but also in all the countries of the Union. More specifically, the institution of the settlement, like the institution of overtime, is two international and certainly European institutions that have been in place for several decades in Europe and Greece. In our country the institution of the settlement has been introduced by the law of 1892/1990 amended with corresponding provisions of law in 1998, 2000, 2005, 2010 and 2011. Also by increasing overtime in 50 hours for both industry and other sectors, thus approaching the European average overtime, the worker is given the opportunity to increase his income. If the worker now opts for the time-setting system - because I stress it is a worker's choice - the additional hours will be stored in a time bank with the contribution of the digital working card and returned to him within six months in the form of a reduced working hours or a day-out or leave days. The profit for the worker is that, through the arrangement, he can guarantee - if he wishes - a four-day working week without a reduction in earnings. As far as the working card is concerned, the worker will be hitting his card when he starts and when he finishes his work. The card will be linked directly to an online system of "ERGANI II" and in this way, in real time, the overtime, overtime and overtime will be recorded and the related consequences, claims for extra pay by the worker and penalties for the employer will immediately be incurred. So the digital card will play a decisive role in the protection of the eighth, in the reduction of black work, in the maintenance of the time of the catcher and in the adjustment of working time, as the locked overtime that the employer has to pay or offset can be recorded online. Any presence at the workplace of a worker who has not activated the digital working card will bring serious administrative sanctions. Also with the draft legislation under discussion, in particular Article 102, the question of the evolution of the Labour Inspectorate into an independent authority is dealt with in order to ensure the effectiveness of public administration in the area of control of compliance with labour and insurance legislation and not to remove the presumption of credibility and objectivity. Of course, the irrationality of counter-population reaches such a point that it even opposes the upgrading of the Labour Inspectorate and its subsequent disconnection from a Ministry of Labour. To conclude, I should like to refer to the strike regulation, first of all, stressing that the right to strike is guaranteed in all the countries of the European Union and is guaranteed in our Constitution and is self-evidently enshrined in the new labour protection bill. However, the new regulations provide for a ban on the revalidation of strikes that have been deemed illegal against the same employer and on the same date as other trade unions with a view to avoiding circumvention of the banning of judicial decisions. In order to protect the rights of citizens and the public interest which outweighs everyone, the minimum guaranteed service of 33% in sectors of general interest, such as transport, waste collection, is also established, i.e. sectors which are of vital importance in meeting the needs of society as a whole. Ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, it is now enough with the distortion to defend the privileges of certain ministries and trade unionists. Put your backs finally for a new Greece that is modern, competitive and adapted to technological economic developments. Thank you.
